{"assignees":[],"body":"## Objective\n\nAdd a `--threshold` CLI flag to `agentv eval` that fails (exit 1) if the mean score across all tests falls below the specified threshold. This enables CI/CD quality gating without needing `agentv compare --baseline`.\n\n## Design Latitude\n\n- Add `--threshold <number>` flag (0-1 scale) to `agentv eval`\n- Also support `execution.threshold` in EVAL.yaml for per-suite defaults\n- CLI flag overrides YAML value\n- After all tests complete, compute mean score; if below threshold, exit code 1 with summary\n- Integrate with existing JUnit XML output (test-level pass/fail based on threshold)\n\n## Acceptance Signals\n\n- `agentv eval evals/ --threshold 0.6` exits 1 if mean score < 0.6\n- `execution.threshold: 0.6` in YAML has the same effect\n- CLI `--threshold` overrides YAML `execution.threshold`\n- Summary line printed: \"Suite score: 0.53 (threshold: 0.6) — FAIL\"\n- Exit code 0 when score meets threshold\n\n## Non-Goals\n\n- Not a replacement for `agentv compare` regression gating (different use case)\n- Not per-test threshold override (use `required` for that)\n- Not severity levels (#334 covers that separately)\n\n## Context\n\nIdentified via [microsoft/skills harness research](https://github.com/agentevals/agentevals-research/blob/main/research/findings/microsoft-skills-harness/agentv-gap-analysis.md). The skills harness uses `--threshold 60` (0-100 scale) for CI quality gates. AgentV currently has per-test `required` gates and `agentv compare --baseline` regression gating, but no suite-level threshold.","labels":[],"title":"feat(cli): --threshold flag for suite-level quality gates"}

It should not change scoring behavior.\n\n## Design Latitude\n\nImplementation details are flexible, but the end state should make `grader` the canonical concept for user-facing scoring primitives.\n\n## Product Decisions\n\n- All user-facing `type:` values represent graders\n- Deprecated `llm-judge` and `code-judge` aliases should be removed\n- User-facing docs, skills, and trace/event naming should prefer `grader` over `evaluator`\n- Internal implementation naming may migrate incrementally, but external terminology should be coherent\n\n## Acceptance Signals\n\n- Deprecated `llm-judge` and `code-judge` values are no longer accepted as canonical type values\n- Type definitions, registries, and parsing paths use `grader` terminology where they model user-facing scoring primitives\n- OTLP event/attribute naming no longer emits new `agentv.evaluator.*` terminology where `agentv.grader.*` is intended\n- Docs and skills consistently describe these primitives as graders\n- Any compatibility path is explicit and documented, or removed entirely\n\n## Non-Goals\n\n- Changing grading semantics or score calculation behavior\n- Redesigning the assertion/grader feature set itself\n- Broad unrelated renames outside the scoring/type-system surface\n\n## Related\n\n- #755\n- #756\n","labels":[],"title":"refactor(grader): standardize user-facing evaluator terminology on grader and remove deprecated *-judge aliases"}

Now let me compile all the findings into a comprehensive summary document:

## Summary: Codebase Research for `--threshold` Flag Design

I've completed a thorough exploration of the AgentV CLI codebase to understand the current evaluation infrastructure. Here's the detailed breakdown:

---

### 1. CLI EVAL COMMAND IMPLEMENTATION

**Location:** `/home/christso/projects/agentv/apps/cli/src/commands/eval/commands/run.ts`

**Command structure:**
- Lines 16-223: Main `evalRunCommand` definition
- Uses `cmd-ts` library for argument parsing
- `handler` (lines 179-222): Delegates to `runEvalCommand()`

**Current flags relevant to evaluation outcomes:**
- `--test-id`: Filter tests by ID pattern (line 35-39)
- `--workers`: Parallel execution control (line 40-45)
- `--output` / `-o`: Multiple output file paths (lines 51-57)
- `--output-format`: jsonl, yaml, or html (lines 58-62)
- `--dry-run`: Mock provider responses (line 63-66)
- `--cache` / `--no-cache`: Response caching (lines 96-103)
- `--verbose`: Verbose logging (line 104-107)
- `--retry-errors`: Re-run only execution_error cases (lines 141-145)
- `--artifacts`: Write companion artifacts (lines 155-160)

**No current exit code logic** at the command handler level—the handler simply awaits `runEvalCommand()` and returns (line 221).

---

### 2. SCORE COMPUTATION & AGGREGATION

**Location:** `/home/christso/projects/agentv/apps/cli/src/commands/eval/statistics.ts`

**Key structures:**

```typescript
// Lines 8-25: EvaluationSummary interface
interface EvaluationSummary {
  readonly total: number;
  readonly mean: number;              // Mean of quality scores
  readonly median: number;
  readonly min: number;
  readonly max: number;
  readonly standardDeviation?: number;
  readonly histogram: readonly HistogramBin[];
  readonly topResults: readonly EvaluationResult[];
  readonly bottomResults: readonly EvaluationResult[];
  readonly errorCount: number;
  readonly executionErrorCount: number;
  readonly qualityFailureCount: number;
  readonly passedCount: number;       // Count with executionStatus === 'ok'
  readonly byFailureStage: Record<string, number>;
  readonly byFailureReason: Record<string, number>;
}
```

**Score computation (lines 85-171):**
- `calculateEvaluationSummary()` computes statistics from `EvaluationResult[]`
- Separates execution errors from quality results (lines 118-120)
- **Mean and median computed from quality scores only** (excluding execution_error results)
- Individual test score is `result.score` (a number, likely 0-1 range)
- `passedCount` = results with `executionStatus === 'ok'` (line 138)
- `qualityFailureCount` = results with `executionStatus === 'quality_failure'` (line 137)
- `executionErrorCount` = results with `executionStatus === 'execution_error'` (line 136)

**Pass/fail threshold for individual tests (lines 197-203):**
- A test "passes" if `executionStatus === 'ok'` (not score-based)
- Pass rate printed as: `${summary.passedCount}/${summary.total} passed`

**Output summary (lines 177-273):**
- `formatEvaluationSummary()` prints color-coded RESULT line (PASS/FAIL)
- Shows mean score, median, min, max, std deviation
- Displays histogram of score distribution [0-0.2, 0.2-0.4, ..., 0.8-1.0]
- Lists top 3 and bottom 3 performing tests

  workers: z.number().int().min(1).max(50).optional(),
  assertions: z.array(EvaluatorSchema).optional(),

```

**Key fields:**
- `trials`: Defines trial count/strategy/cost limits (lines 307-312)
- `fail_on_error`: Boolean flag to fail on execution errors (line 315)
- `total_budget_usd`: Suite-level cost limit
- No score threshold field exists in the schema

**Full eval file top-level (lines 359-390):**

- `tests`: Array of test cases or path to external file (line 377)
- Individual tests can override execution settings (line 347)

### 4. JUNIT XML OUTPUT

**Location:** `/home/christso/projects/agentv/apps/cli/src/commands/eval/junit-writer.ts`

**Pass/fail determination (lines 55-76):**

```typescript
const failures = results.filter((r) => r.score < 0.5).length;  // Line 55
const errors = results.filter((r) => r.error !== undefined).length;  // Line 56

// Lines 62-75: Per-testcase XML generation
if (r.error) {
  // <error> tag
} else if (r.score < 0.5) {
  // <failure> tag with message "score=X.XXX"
}
```

**Hardcoded threshold: `score < 0.5` is treated as a failure**
- Tests with error → `<error>` element
- Tests with `score < 0.5` → `<failure>` element
- Tests with `score >= 0.5` → pass (no inner element)

**JUnit output structure:**
- Groups results by `eval_set` into `<testsuite>` elements
- Each `<testcase>` includes name, classname, time
- Summary attributes: `tests`, `failures`, `errors`

### 5. SUMMARY & REPORTING

**Location:** `/home/christso/projects/agentv/apps/cli/src/commands/eval/run-eval.ts`

**Summary output flow (lines 1152-1157):**

```typescript
const summary = calculateEvaluationSummary(allResults);  // Line 1152
console.log(formatEvaluationSummary(summary));           // Line 1153

// Matrix summary for multi-target evals (lines 1156-1158)
if (isMatrixMode && allResults.length > 0) {
  console.log(formatMatrixSummary(allResults));
}
```

**Verdict determination (statistics.ts, lines 197-203):**

```typescript
const overallPassed =
  summary.passedCount === summary.total - summary.executionErrorCount ||
  (summary.qualityFailureCount === 0 && summary.executionErrorCount === 0);
const overallVerdict = overallPassed ? 'PASS' : 'FAIL';
```

**Logic:** Overall PASS if:
1. All non-execution-error tests passed, OR
2. No quality failures AND no execution errors

### 6. EXIT CODE HANDLING

**Current status:** **No exit code enforcement in `agentv eval run`**

**Location:** `/home/christso/projects/agentv/apps/cli/src/commands/eval/commands/run.ts`
- Lines 179-222: handler simply awaits `runEvalCommand()` and returns
- No `process.exit()` or `process.exitCode` set

**Comparison with `agentv eval assert`** (lines 95-108 in `/home/christso/projects/agentv/apps/cli/src/commands/eval/commands/assert.ts`):
- `process.exit(score >= 0.5 ? 0 : 1)` — exits with code 1 if score < 0.5
- This is the only eval subcommand with exit code logic

**Return structure (lines 747-752):**

```typescript
export interface RunEvalResult {
  readonly executionErrorCount: number;
  readonly outputPath: string;
  readonly testFiles: readonly string[];
  readonly target?: string;
}
```

Result is returned but **not used for exit code decision**. The execution error count is available but currently ignored.

---

### 7. EVALUATION RESULT TYPE

**Location:** `/home/christso/projects/agentv/packages/core/src/evaluation/types.ts`

**Key fields (lines 902-966):**

```typescript
export interface EvaluationResult {
  readonly testId: string;
  readonly score: number;                    // Main quality score
  readonly assertions: readonly AssertionEntry[];
  readonly error?: string;                   // Execution error (if any)
  readonly executionStatus: ExecutionStatus; // 'ok' | 'quality_failure' | 'execution_error'
  readonly failureStage?: FailureStage;      // Where failure occurred
  readonly failureReasonCode?: string;       // Machine-readable reason
  readonly scores?: readonly EvaluatorResult[]; // Per-evaluator breakdowns
  // ... other fields (duration, cost, traces, workspace path, etc.)
}

export type ExecutionStatus = 'ok' | 'quality_failure' | 'execution_error';  // Line 877
```

**Score semantics:**
- Individual scores range from 0-1
- No hard threshold; interpretation is display-only (except JUnit's 0.5 hardcoding)
- Multiple evaluators can contribute to a single result score via composition

---

### 8. OUTPUT FORMATS SUPPORTED

**Location:** `/home/christso/projects/agentv/apps/cli/src/commands/eval/output-writer.ts`

Supported formats (lines 36-57):
- `.jsonl` → JSONL writer (default, one result per line)
- `.json` → JSON writer (array of results)
- `.xml` → JUnit writer (XML format)
- `.yaml` / `.yml` → YAML writer
- `.html` / `.htm` → HTML report writer

**Multi-format output:** `--output` flag can be repeated to write to multiple formats simultaneously (line 60-70).

---

## Key Insights for `--threshold` Design

1. **Currently hardcoded thresholds exist:**
   - JUnit XML: `score < 0.5` = failure
   - Test verdict: `executionStatus === 'ok'` (binary, not score-based)
   - No configurable threshold in code

2. **Score range:** Tests use a 0-1 scale (based on evaluator scoring)

3. **No current exit code logic:** `agentv eval run` doesn't set process.exit() based on results

4. **Multiple evaluation status dimensions:**
   - `executionStatus`: 'ok' | 'quality_failure' | 'execution_error'
   - `score`: 0-1 numeric value
   - `passedCount`: binary count of 'ok' status

5. **Summary available:** `calculateEvaluationSummary()` provides:
   - `passedCount`: number passing
   - `mean`: average score (quality tests only)
   - `qualityFailureCount`: count of quality_failure status
   - `executionErrorCount`: count of execution_error status

6. **CLI architecture:** The `run.ts` handler doesn't process results, just delegates and returns. Exit code logic would need to be added here or in `run-eval.ts`.

> AGENT

Now let me propose approaches. Given the clear scope from the issue and your answers, here are two approaches:

## Approach A: Suite-level threshold only (Recommended)

- `--threshold <0-1>` on CLI, `execution.threshold` in YAML
- After all tests complete, compute mean score (quality results only)
- If mean < threshold → print summary line, exit 1
- JUnit writer uses threshold for per-test pass/fail (defaulting to 0.5 if unset)
- Single threshold value, simple mental model

## Approach B: Suite + per-test threshold

- Same as A, but also add per-test `threshold` in YAML test config
- Each test's score is checked individually against its threshold
- More flexible but adds complexity, and the issue explicitly says "not per-test threshold override"

**Recommendation: Approach A.** The issue says per-test threshold is a non-goal (use `required` for that). Suite-level only keeps it simple and covers the CI quality gate use case.

> AGENT

## Design: `--threshold` flag for suite-level quality gates

### CLI Flag

Add `--threshold <number>` to `agentv eval run`:
- Accepts a number in 0–1 range (e.g., `--threshold 0.8`)
- Optional — if omitted, no threshold check is performed (current behavior)
- Overrides `execution.threshold` from YAML if both are set

### YAML Config

Add `threshold` to the `execution` block:

```yaml
execution:
  threshold: 0.8
```

### Score Evaluation

After all tests complete:
1. Compute mean score from quality results only (excluding `execution_error` tests — same as current `calculateEvaluationSummary()`)
2. If mean score < threshold → exit code 1
3. Execution errors fail independently via existing `fail_on_error` mechanism
4. If no quality results exist (all execution errors), threshold check is skipped

### Output

When threshold is active, append a summary line:
```
Suite score: 0.53 (threshold: 0.60) — FAIL
```
or
```
Suite score: 0.85 (threshold: 0.60) — PASS
```

### JUnit Integration

The JUnit writer uses the threshold for per-test pass/fail:
- If threshold is set, `score < threshold` → `<failure>`
- If threshold is not set, falls back to `score < 0.5` (current behavior)

### Exit Code

- Exit 0: mean score >= threshold (or no threshold set)
- Exit 1: mean score < threshold
- Execution errors handled separately by `fail_on_error`

Does this design look right?

  "content": "# Design: `--threshold` flag for suite-level quality gates\n\n**Issue:** #698\n**Date:** 2026-03-25\n\n## Objective\n\nAdd a `--threshold` CLI flag to `agentv eval` that fails (exit 1) if the mean score across all tests falls below the specified threshold. This enables CI/CD quality gating without needing `agentv compare --baseline`.\n\n## CLI Flag\n\n- `--threshold <number>` on `agentv eval run` (0–1 scale)\n- Optional — if omitted, no threshold check (current behavior preserved)\n- Overrides `execution.threshold` from YAML if both set\n\n## YAML Config\n\nAdd `threshold` to the `execution` block in eval YAML files:\n\n```yaml\nexecution:\n  threshold: 0.8\n```\n\nBoth `threshold` and `execution.threshold` accepted (snake_case wire format convention).\n\n## Score Evaluation\n\nAfter all tests complete:\n\n1. Compute mean score from quality results only (excluding `execution_error` tests — same as existing `calculateEvaluationSummary()`)\n2. If mean score < threshold → exit code 1\n3. If no quality results exist (all execution errors), threshold check is skipped\n\n## Output\n\nWhen threshold is active, append a summary line after the existing result summary:\n\n```\nSuite score: 0.53 (threshold: 0.60) — FAIL\n```\n\nor:\n\n```\nSuite score: 0.85 (threshold: 0.60) — PASS\n```\n\n## JUnit Integration\n\nThe JUnit writer uses the threshold for per-test pass/fail:\n\n- If threshold is set: `score < threshold` → `<failure>` element\n- If threshold is not set: `score < 0.5` (current hardcoded behavior preserved)\n\n## Exit Code\n\n- Exit 0: mean score >= threshold (or no threshold set)\n- Exit 1: mean score < threshold\n- Execution errors handled separately by `fail_on_error`\n\n## Files to Modify\n\n1. `packages/core/src/evaluation/validation/eval-file.schema.ts` — add `threshold` to ExecutionSchema\n2. `apps/cli/src/commands/eval/commands/run.ts` — add `--threshold` CLI flag\n3. `apps/cli/src/commands/eval/run-eval.ts` — pass threshold through, check after results\n4. `apps/cli/src/commands/eval/statistics.ts` — add threshold summary formatting\n5. `apps/cli/src/commands/eval/junit-writer.ts` — use threshold for pass/fail\n6.    218→      model: args.model,
   219→      outputMessages: args.outputMessages,

     1→/**
     2→ * Zod schema for eval YAML file format.
     3→ * Used to generate eval-schema.json for AI agent reference.
     4→ *
     5→ * IMPORTANT: This schema describes the YAML input format, not the parsed runtime types.
     6→ * When adding new eval features, update this schema AND run `bun run generate:schema`
     7→ * to regenerate eval-schema.json. The sync test will fail if they diverge.
     8→ */
     9→import { z } from 'zod';
    10→
    11→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    12→// Shared primitives
    13→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    14→
    15→/** Message content: string or structured array */
    16→const ContentItemSchema = z.object({
    17→  type: z.enum(['text', 'file']),
    18→  value: z.string(),
    19→});
    20→
    21→const MessageContentSchema = z.union([z.string(), z.array(ContentItemSchema)]);
    22→
    23→const MessageSchema = z.object({
    24→  role: z.enum(['system', 'user', 'assistant', 'tool']),
    25→  content: MessageContentSchema,
    26→});
    27→
    28→/** Input: string shorthand or message array */
    29→const InputSchema = z.union([z.string(), z.array(MessageSchema)]);
    30→
    31→/** Expected output: string, object, or message array */
    32→const ExpectedOutputSchema = z.union([z.string(), z.record(z.unknown()), z.array(MessageSchema)]);
    33→
    34→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    35→// Evaluator schemas (YAML input format)
    36→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    37→
    38→/** Common fields shared by all evaluators */
    39→const EvaluatorCommonSchema = z.object({
    40→  name: z.string().optional(),
    41→  weight: z.number().min(0).optional(),
    42→  required: z.union([z.boolean(), z.number().gt(0).lte(1)]).optional(),
    43→  negate: z.boolean().optional(),
    44→});
    45→
    46→/** Prompt: string (inline/file path) or executable script config */
    47→const PromptSchema = z.union([
    48→  z.string(),
    49→  z.object({
    50→    command: z.union([z.string(), z.array(z.string())]).optional(),
    51→    script: z.union([z.string(), z.array(z.string())]).optional(),
    52→    config: z.record(z.unknown()).optional(),
    53→  }),
    54→]);
    55→
    56→/** Score range for analytic rubrics */
    57→const ScoreRangeSchema = z.object({
    58→  score_range: z.tuple([z.number().int().min(0).max(10), z.number().int().min(0).max(10)]),
    59→  outcome: z.string().min(1),
    60→});
    61→
    62→/** Rubric item (checklist or score-range mode) */
    63→const RubricItemSchema = z.object({
    64→  id: z.string().optional(),
    65→  outcome: z.string().optional(),
    66→  weight: z.number().optional(),
    67→  required: z.boolean().optional(),
    68→  required_min_score: z.number().int().min(0).max(10).optional(),
    69→  score_ranges: z.array(ScoreRangeSchema).optional(),
    70→});
    71→
    72→// --- Type-specific evaluator schemas ---
    73→
    74→const CodeGraderSchema = EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({
    75→  type: z.enum(['code-grader', 'code_grader', 'code-judge', 'code_judge']),
    76→  command: z.union([z.string(), z.array(z.string())]),
    77→  script: z.union([z.string(), z.array(z.string())]).optional(),
    78→  cwd: z.string().optional(),
    79→  target: z.union([z.boolean(), z.object({ max_calls: z.number().optional() })]).optional(),
    80→  config: z.record(z.unknown()).optional(),
    81→});
    82→
    83→const LlmGraderSchema = EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({
    84→  type: z.enum(['llm-grader', 'llm_grader', 'llm-judge', 'llm_judge']),
    85→  prompt: PromptSchema.optional(),
    86→  rubrics: z.array(RubricItemSchema).optional(),
    87→  model: z.string().optional(),
    88→  target: z.string().optional(),
    89→  config: z.record(z.unknown()).optional(),
    90→  max_steps: z.number().int().min(1).max(50).optional(),
    91→  temperature: z.number().min(0).max(2).optional(),
    92→});
    93→
    94→/** Aggregator configs for composite evaluator */
    95→const AggregatorSchema = z.discriminatedUnion('type', [
    96→  z.object({
    97→    type: z.literal('weighted_average'),
    98→    weights: z.record(z.number()).optional(),
    99→  }),
   100→  z.object({
   101→    type: z.literal('threshold'),
   102→    threshold: z.number().min(0).max(1),
   103→  }),
   104→  z.object({
   105→    type: z.literal('code-judge'),
   106→    path: z.string(),
   107→    cwd: z.string().optional(),
   108→  }),
   109→  z.object({
   110→    type: z.literal('llm-judge'),
   111→    prompt: z.string().optional(),
   112→    model: z.string().optional(),
   113→  }),
   114→]);
   115→
   116→// Use z.lazy for recursive composite evaluator
   117→const CompositeSchema: z.ZodType = z.lazy(() =>
   118→  EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({
   119→    type: z.literal('composite'),
   120→    assertions: z.array(EvaluatorSchema).optional(),
   121→    assert: z.array(EvaluatorSchema).optional(),
   122→    evaluators: z.array(EvaluatorSchema).optional(),
   123→    aggregator: AggregatorSchema,
   124→  }),
   125→);
   126→

   128→  z.enum(['exact', 'ignore', 'subset', 'superset']),
   129→  z.array(z.string()),
   130→]);
   131→
   132→const ToolTrajectoryExpectedItemSchema = z.object({
   133→  tool: z.string(),
   134→  args: z.union([z.literal('any'), z.record(z.unknown())]).optional(),
   135→  max_duration_ms: z.number().min(0).optional(),
   136→  maxDurationMs: z.number().min(0).optional(),
   137→  args_match: ArgsMatchSchema.optional(),
   138→  argsMatch: ArgsMatchSchema.optional(),
   139→});
   140→
   141→const ToolTrajectorySchema = EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({
   142→  type: z.enum(['tool-trajectory', 'tool_trajectory']),
   143→  mode: z.enum(['any_order', 'in_order', 'exact', 'subset', 'superset']),
   144→  minimums: z.record(z.number().int().min(0)).optional(),
   145→  expected: z.array(ToolTrajectoryExpectedItemSchema).optional(),
   146→  args_match: ArgsMatchSchema.optional(),
   147→  argsMatch: ArgsMatchSchema.optional(),
   148→});
   149→
   150→const FieldConfigSchema = z.object({
   151→  path: z.string(),
   152→  match: z.enum(['exact', 'numeric_tolerance', 'date']),
   153→  required: z.boolean().optional(),
   154→  weight: z.number().optional(),
   155→  tolerance: z.number().min(0).optional(),
   156→  relative: z.boolean().optional(),

   158→});
   159→
   160→const FieldAccuracySchema = EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({
   161→  type: z.enum(['field-accuracy', 'field_accuracy']),
   162→  fields: z.array(FieldConfigSchema).min(1),
   163→  aggregation: z.enum(['weighted_average', 'all_or_nothing']).optional(),

   165→
   166→const LatencySchema = EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({
   167→  type: z.literal('latency'),
   168→  threshold: z.number().min(0),

   170→
   171→const CostSchema = EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({

   175→
   176→const TokenUsageSchema = EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({
   177→  type: z.enum(['token-usage', 'token_usage']),
   178→  max_total: z.number().min(0).optional(),
   179→  max_input: z.number().min(0).optional(),
   180→  max_output: z.number().min(0).optional(),
   181→});
   182→
   183→const ExecutionMetricsSchema = EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({
   184→  type: z.enum(['execution-metrics', 'execution_metrics']),
   185→  max_tool_calls: z.number().min(0).optional(),
   186→  max_llm_calls: z.number().min(0).optional(),
   187→  max_tokens: z.number().min(0).optional(),
   188→  max_cost_usd: z.number().min(0).optional(),
   189→  max_duration_ms: z.number().min(0).optional(),
   190→  target_exploration_ratio: z.number().min(0).max(1).optional(),
   191→  exploration_tolerance: z.number().min(0).optional(),
   192→});
   193→
   194→const ContainsSchema = EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({
   195→  type: z.literal('contains'),
   196→  value: z.string(),
   197→});
   198→
   199→const RegexSchema = EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({
   200→  type: z.literal('regex'),
   201→  value: z.string(),
   202→});
   203→
   204→const IsJsonSchema = EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({
   205→  type: z.enum(['is-json', 'is_json']),
   206→});
   207→
   208→const EqualsSchema = EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({
   209→  type: z.literal('equals'),

   211→});
   212→
   213→const RubricsSchema = EvaluatorCommonSchema.extend({
   214→  type: z.literal('rubrics'),

   216→});
   217→
   218→/** Union of all evaluator types */
   219→const EvaluatorSchema = z.union([

   236→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

    14→  readonly standardDeviation?: number;
    15→  readonly histogram: readonly HistogramBin[];
    16→  readonly topResults: readonly EvaluationResult[];
    17→  readonly bottomResults: readonly EvaluationResult[];
    18→  readonly errorCount: number;
    19→  readonly errors: readonly { readonly testId: string; readonly error: string }[];
    20→  readonly executionErrorCount: number;
    21→  readonly qualityFailureCount: number;
    22→  readonly passedCount: number;
    23→  readonly byFailureStage: Readonly<Record<string, number>>;
    24→  readonly byFailureReason: Readonly<Record<string, number>>;
    25→}
    26→
    27→const HISTOGRAM_BREAKPOINTS = [0, 0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 1];
    28→
    29→function computeMean(values: readonly number[]): number {
    30→  if (values.length === 0) {
    31→    return 0;
    32→  }
    33→  const sum = values.reduce((acc, value) => acc + value, 0);
    34→  return sum / values.length;
    35→}
    36→
    37→function computeMedian(values: readonly number[]): number {
    38→  if (values.length === 0) {
    39→    return 0;
    40→  }
    41→  const sorted = [...values].sort((a, b) => a - b);
    42→  const mid = Math.floor(sorted.length / 2);
    43→  if (sorted.length % 2 === 0) {
    44→    return (sorted[mid - 1] + sorted[mid]) / 2;
    45→  }
    46→  return sorted[mid];
    47→}
    48→
    49→function computeStandardDeviation(values: readonly number[]): number | undefined {
    50→  if (values.length < 2) {
    51→    return undefined;
    52→  }
    53→  const mean = computeMean(values);
    54→  const variance =
    55→    values.reduce((acc, value) => acc + (value - mean) ** 2, 0) / (values.length - 1);
    56→  return Math.sqrt(variance);
    57→}
    58→
    59→function buildHistogram(values: readonly number[]): readonly HistogramBin[] {
    60→  const bins: HistogramBin[] = [];
    61→  for (let index = 0; index < HISTOGRAM_BREAKPOINTS.length - 1; index += 1) {
    62→    bins.push({
    63→      range: [HISTOGRAM_BREAKPOINTS[index], HISTOGRAM_BREAKPOINTS[index + 1]] as const,
    64→      count: 0,
    65→    });
    66→  }
    67→
    68→  for (const value of values) {
    69→    for (const bin of bins) {
    70→      const [start, end] = bin.range;
    71→      const isLastBin = end === HISTOGRAM_BREAKPOINTS[HISTOGRAM_BREAKPOINTS.length - 1];
    72→      const withinRange = isLastBin
    73→        ? value >= start && value <= end
    74→        : value >= start && value < end + 1e-9;
    75→      if (withinRange) {
    76→        bin.count += 1;
    77→        break;
    78→      }
    79→    }
    80→  }
    81→
    82→  return bins;
    83→}
    84→
    85→export function calculateEvaluationSummary(
    86→  results: readonly EvaluationResult[],
    87→): EvaluationSummary {
    88→  const total = results.length;
    89→
    90→  // Track errors
    91→  const errors = results
    92→    .filter((result) => result.error !== undefined)
    93→    .map((result) => ({ testId: result.testId, error: result.error as string }));
    94→  const errorCount = errors.length;
    95→
    96→  if (total === 0) {
    97→    return {
    98→      total: 0,
    99→      mean: 0,
   100→      median: 0,
   101→      min: 0,
   102→      max: 0,
   103→      standardDeviation: undefined,
   104→      histogram: buildHistogram([]),
   105→      topResults: [],
   106→      bottomResults: [],
   107→      errorCount: 0,
   108→      errors: [],
   109→      executionErrorCount: 0,
   110→      qualityFailureCount: 0,
   111→      passedCount: 0,
   112→      byFailureStage: {},
   113→      byFailureReason: {},
   114→    };
   115→  }
   116→
   117→  // Separate execution errors from quality results
   118→  const executionErrors = results.filter((r) => r.executionStatus === 'execution_error');
   119→  const qualityResults = results.filter((r) => r.executionStatus !== 'execution_error');
   120→  const qualityScores = qualityResults.map((r) => r.score);
   121→
   122→  // Compute quality metrics from non-execution-error results only
   123→  const mean = computeMean(qualityScores);
   124→  const median = computeMedian(qualityScores);
   125→  const min = qualityScores.length > 0 ? Math.min(...qualityScores) : 0;
   126→  const max = qualityScores.length > 0 ? Math.max(...qualityScores) : 0;
   127→  const standardDeviation = computeStandardDeviation(qualityScores);
   128→  const histogram = buildHistogram(qualityScores);
   129→
   130→  // Top/bottom results consider only non-execution-error results
   131→  const sortedResults = [...qualityResults].sort((a, b) => b.score - a.score);
   132→  const topResults = sortedResults.slice(0, Math.min(3, sortedResults.length));
   133→  const bottomResults = sortedResults.slice(-Math.min(3, sortedResults.length));
   134→
   135→  // Count by execution status
   136→  const executionErrorCount = executionErrors.length;
   137→  const qualityFailureCount = results.filter((r) => r.executionStatus === 'quality_failure').length;
   138→  const passedCount = results.filter((r) => r.executionStatus === 'ok').length;
   139→
   140→  // Aggregate by failure stage and reason (execution errors only)
   141→  const byFailureStage: Record<string, number> = {};
   142→  const byFailureReason: Record<string, number> = {};
   143→  for (const result of executionErrors) {
   144→    if (result.failureStage) {
   145→      byFailureStage[result.failureStage] = (byFailureStage[result.failureStage] ?? 0) + 1;
   146→    }
   147→    if (result.failureReasonCode) {
   148→      byFailureReason[result.failureReasonCode] =
   149→        (byFailureReason[result.failureReasonCode] ?? 0) + 1;
   150→    }
   151→  }
   152→
   153→  return {
   154→    total,
   155→    mean,
   156→    median,
   157→    min,
   158→    max,
   159→    standardDeviation,
   160→    histogram,
   161→    topResults,
   162→    bottomResults,
   163→    errorCount,
   164→    errors,
   165→    executionErrorCount,
   166→    qualityFailureCount,
   167→    passedCount,
   168→    byFailureStage,
   169→    byFailureReason,
   170→  };
   171→}
   172→
   173→function formatScore(value: number): string {
   174→  return value.toFixed(3);
   175→}
   176→
   177→export function formatEvaluationSummary(summary: EvaluationSummary): string {
   178→  if (summary.total === 0) {
   179→    return '\nNo results to summarize';
   180→  }
   181→
   182→  const lines: string[] = [];
   183→
   184→  // Display errors first if any exist
   185→  if (summary.errorCount > 0) {
   186→    lines.push('\n==================================================');
   187→    lines.push('EXECUTION ERRORS');
   188→    lines.push('==================================================');
   189→    for (const error of summary.errors) {
   190→      lines.push(`\n❌ ${error.testId}`);
   191→      lines.push(`   ${error.error}`);
   192→    }
   193→    lines.push('');
   194→  }
   195→
   196→  // Overall verdict line
   197→  const overallPassed =
   198→    summary.passedCount === summary.total - summary.executionErrorCount ||
   199→    (summary.qualityFailureCount === 0 && summary.executionErrorCount === 0);
   200→  const overallVerdict = overallPassed ? 'PASS' : 'FAIL';
   201→  const useColor = !(process.env.NO_COLOR !== undefined) && (process.stdout.isTTY ?? false);
   202→  const verdictColor = overallPassed ? '\x1b[32m' : '\x1b[31m';
   203→  const verdictText = `RESULT: ${overallVerdict}  (${summary.passedCount}/${summary.total} passed, mean score: ${formatScore(summary.mean)})`;
   204→
   205→  lines.push('\n==================================================');
   206→  if (useColor) {
   207→    lines.push(`\x1b[1m${verdictColor}${verdictText}\x1b[0m`);
   208→  } else {
   209→    lines.push(verdictText);
   210→  }
   211→  lines.push('==================================================');
   212→
   213→  lines.push('');
   214→  lines.push('EVALUATION SUMMARY');
   215→  lines.push('==================================================');
   216→  lines.push(`Total tests: ${summary.total}`);
   217→  lines.push(`Passed: ${summary.passedCount}`);
   218→  if (summary.qualityFailureCount > 0) {
   219→    lines.push(`Quality failures: ${summary.qualityFailureCount}`);
   220→  }
   221→  if (summary.executionErrorCount > 0) {
   222→    lines.push(`Execution errors: ${summary.executionErrorCount}`);
   223→  }
   224→
   225→  if (summary.executionErrorCount > 0) {
   226→    const qualityCount = summary.total - summary.executionErrorCount;
   227→    lines.push(
   228→      `Mean score: ${formatScore(summary.mean)} (${qualityCount} quality tests, ${summary.executionErrorCount} execution errors excluded)`,
   229→    );
   230→  } else {
   231→    lines.push(`Mean score: ${formatScore(summary.mean)}`);
   232→  }
   233→  lines.push(`Median score: ${formatScore(summary.median)}`);
   234→  lines.push(`Min score: ${formatScore(summary.min)}`);
   235→  lines.push(`Max score: ${formatScore(summary.max)}`);
   236→  if (typeof summary.standardDeviation === 'number') {
   237→    lines.push(`Std deviation: ${formatScore(summary.standardDeviation)}`);
   238→  }
   239→
   240→  lines.push('\nScore distribution:');
   241→  for (const bin of summary.histogram) {
   242→    const [start, end] = bin.range;
   243→    lines.push(`  ${start.toFixed(1)}-${end.toFixed(1)}: ${bin.count}`);
   244→  }
   245→
   246→  lines.push('\nTop performing tests:');
   247→  summary.topResults.forEach((result, index) => {
   248→    lines.push(`  ${index + 1}. ${result.testId}: ${formatScore(result.score)}`);
   249→  });
   250→
   251→  lines.push('\nLowest performing tests:');
   252→  summary.bottomResults.forEach((result, index) => {
   253→    lines.push(`  ${index + 1}. ${result.testId}: ${formatScore(result.score)}`);
   254→  });
   255→
   256→  const failureStageEntries = Object.entries(summary.byFailureStage);
   257→  if (failureStageEntries.length > 0) {
   258→    lines.push('\nExecution errors by stage:');
   259→    for (const [stage, count] of failureStageEntries) {
   260→      lines.push(`  ${stage}: ${count}`);
   261→    }
   262→  }
   263→
   264→  const failureReasonEntries = Object.entries(summary.byFailureReason);
   265→  if (failureReasonEntries.length > 0) {
   266→    lines.push('\nExecution errors by reason:');
   267→    for (const [reason, count] of failureReasonEntries) {
   268→      lines.push(`  ${reason}: ${count}`);
   269→    }
   270→  }
   271→
   272→  return lines.join('\n');
   273→}
   274→
   275→/**
   276→ * Format a matrix summary table showing tests × targets.
   277→ */
   278→export function formatMatrixSummary(results: readonly EvaluationResult[]): string {
   279→  // Collect unique targets and test IDs
   280→  const targetSet = new Set<string>();
   281→  const testIdSet = new Set<string>();
   282→  for (const result of results) {
   283→    targetSet.add(result.target);
   284→    testIdSet.add(result.testId);
   285→  }
   286→
   287→  const targets = [...targetSet].sort();
   288→  const testIds = [...testIdSet].sort();
   289→
   290→  if (targets.length < 2) {
   291→    return '';
   292→  }
   293→
   294→  // Build lookup: testId -> target -> score
   295→  const scoreMap = new Map<string, Map<string, number>>();
   296→  for (const result of results) {
   297→    if (!scoreMap.has(result.testId)) {
   298→      scoreMap.set(result.testId, new Map());
   299→    }
   300→    scoreMap.get(result.testId)?.set(result.target, result.score);
   301→  }
   302→
   303→  const lines: string[] = [];
   304→  lines.push('\n==================================================');
   305→  lines.push('MATRIX RESULTS (tests × targets)');
   306→  lines.push('==================================================');
   307→
   308→  // Header row
   309→  const testIdColWidth = Math.max(7, ...testIds.map((id) => id.length));
   310→  const targetColWidth = Math.max(7, ...targets.map((t) => t.length));
   311→  const header = `${'Test'.padEnd(testIdColWidth)}  ${targets.map((t) => t.padEnd(targetColWidth)).join('  ')}`;
   312→  lines.push(header);
   313→  lines.push('-'.repeat(header.length));
   314→
   315→  // Data rows
   316→  for (const testId of testIds) {
   317→    const cells = targets.map((target) => {
   318→      const score = scoreMap.get(testId)?.get(target);
   319→      return score !== undefined
   320→        ? formatScore(score).padEnd(targetColWidth)
   321→        : '-'.padEnd(targetColWidth);
   322→    });
   323→    lines.push(`${testId.padEnd(testIdColWidth)}  ${cells.join('  ')}`);
   324→  }
   325→
   326→  // Per-target averages
   327→  lines.push('-'.repeat(header.length));
   328→  const avgCells = targets.map((target) => {
   329→    const scores = results.filter((r) => r.target === target).map((r) => r.score);
   330→    const avg = scores.length > 0 ? scores.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / scores.length : 0;
   331→    return formatScore(avg).padEnd(targetColWidth);
   332→  });
   333→  lines.push(`${'Average'.padEnd(testIdColWidth)}  ${avgCells.join('  ')}`);
   334→
   335→  return lines.join('\n');
   336→}
   337→

     1→import { mkdir, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
     2→import path from 'node:path';
     3→
     4→import type { EvaluationResult } from '@agentv/core';
     5→
     6→export function escapeXml(str: string): string {
     7→  return str
     8→    .replace(/&/g, '&amp;')
     9→    .replace(/</g, '&lt;')
    10→    .replace(/>/g, '&gt;')
    11→    .replace(/"/g, '&quot;')
    12→    .replace(/'/g, '&apos;');
    13→}
    14→
    15→export class JunitWriter {
    16→  private readonly filePath: string;
    17→  private readonly results: EvaluationResult[] = [];
    18→  private closed = false;
    19→
    20→  private constructor(filePath: string) {
    21→    this.filePath = filePath;
    22→  }
    23→
    24→  static async open(filePath: string): Promise<JunitWriter> {
    25→    await mkdir(path.dirname(filePath), { recursive: true });
    26→    return new JunitWriter(filePath);
    27→  }
    28→
    29→  async append(result: EvaluationResult): Promise<void> {
    30→    if (this.closed) {
    31→      throw new Error('Cannot write to closed JUnit writer');
    32→    }
    33→    this.results.push(result);
    34→  }
    35→
    36→  async close(): Promise<void> {
    37→    if (this.closed) {
    38→      return;
    39→    }
    40→    this.closed = true;
    41→
    42→    const grouped = new Map<string, EvaluationResult[]>();
    43→    for (const result of this.results) {
    44→      const suite = result.eval_set ?? 'default';
    45→      const existing = grouped.get(suite);
    46→      if (existing) {
    47→        existing.push(result);
    48→      } else {
    49→        grouped.set(suite, [result]);
    50→      }
    51→    }
    52→
    53→    const suiteXmls: string[] = [];
    54→    for (const [suiteName, results] of grouped) {
    55→      const failures = results.filter((r) => r.score < 0.5).length;
    56→      const errors = results.filter((r) => r.error !== undefined).length;
    57→
    58→      const testCases = results.map((r) => {
    59→        const time = r.durationMs ? (r.durationMs / 1000).toFixed(3) : '0.000';
    60→
    61→        let inner = '';
    62→        if (r.error) {
    63→          inner = `\n      <error message="${escapeXml(r.error)}">${escapeXml(r.error)}</error>\n    `;
    64→        } else if (r.score < 0.5) {
    65→          const message = `score=${r.score.toFixed(3)}`;
    66→          const failedAssertions = r.assertions.filter((a) => !a.passed);
    67→          const detail = [
    68→            `Score: ${r.score.toFixed(3)}`,
    69→            failedAssertions.length > 0
    70→              ? `Failed: ${failedAssertions.map((a) => a.text).join(', ')}`
    71→              : '',
    72→          ]
    73→            .filter(Boolean)
    74→            .join('\n');
    75→          inner = `\n      <failure message="${escapeXml(message)}">${escapeXml(detail)}</failure>\n    `;
    76→        }
    77→
    78→        return `    <testcase name="${escapeXml(r.testId)}" classname="${escapeXml(suiteName)}" time="${time}">${inner}</testcase>`;
    79→      });
    80→
    81→      suiteXmls.push(
    82→        `  <testsuite name="${escapeXml(suiteName)}" tests="${results.length}" failures="${failures}" errors="${errors}">\n${testCases.join('\n')}\n  </testsuite>`,
    83→      );
    84→    }
    85→
    86→    const totalTests = this.results.length;
    87→    const totalFailures = this.results.filter((r) => r.score < 0.5).length;
    88→    const totalErrors = this.results.filter((r) => r.error !== undefined).length;
    89→
    90→    const xml = `<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>\n<testsuites tests="${totalTests}" failures="${totalFailures}" errors="${totalErrors}">\n${suiteXmls.join('\n')}\n</testsuites>\n`;
    91→
    92→    await writeFile(this.filePath, xml, 'utf8');
    93→  }
    94→}
    95→

   103→export function resolveTimestampPlaceholder(value: string): string {
   104→  if (!value.includes('{timestamp}')) {
   105→    return value;
   106→  }
   107→  const timestamp = new Date().toISOString().replace(/[:.]/g, '-');
   108→  return value.replaceAll('{timestamp}', timestamp);

   111→function normalizeNumber(value: unknown, fallback: number): number {
   112→  if (typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value)) {
   113→    return value;
   114→  }
   115→  if (typeof value === 'string') {
   116→    const parsed = Number.parseInt(value, 10);
   117→    if (!Number.isNaN(parsed)) {
   118→      return parsed;
   119→    }
   120→  }
   121→  return fallback;

   142→ * Normalize --output-messages value. Accepts a number (>= 1) or "all".
   143→ * Defaults to 1 (last assistant message only).
   144→ */
   145→function normalizeOutputMessages(cliValue: string | undefined): number | 'all' {
   146→  if (cliValue === undefined) {
   147→    return 1;
   148→  }
   149→  if (cliValue === 'all') {
   150→    return 'all';
   151→  }
   152→  const parsed = Number.parseInt(cliValue, 10);
   153→  if (Number.isNaN(parsed) || !Number.isInteger(parsed) || parsed < 1) {
   154→    console.warn(
   155→      `Warning: Invalid --output-messages value '${cliValue}'. Must be a positive integer or 'all'. Defaulting to 1.`,
   156→    );
   157→    return 1;
   158→  }
   159→  return parsed;
   160→}
   161→
   162→/**
   163→ * Trim output messages for results JSONL.
   164→ * Each message is stripped to { role, content } only.
   165→ *
   166→ * - `1` (default): last assistant message only (legacy behavior)
   167→ * - `N`: last N messages (any role)
   168→ * - `'all'`: all messages
   169→ */
   170→export function trimOutputMessages(
   171→  output: EvaluationResult['output'],
   172→  outputMessages: number | 'all',
   173→): EvaluationResult['output'] {
   174→  const messages = output ?? [];
   175→
   176→  if (outputMessages === 'all') {
   177→    return messages.map((m) => ({ role: m.role, content: m.content }));
   178→  }
   179→
   180→  if (outputMessages === 1) {
   181→    // Legacy behavior: last assistant message only
   182→    const lastAssistant = messages.filter((m) => m.role === 'assistant').at(-1);
   183→    return lastAssistant ? [{ role: lastAssistant.role, content: lastAssistant.content }] : [];
   184→  }
   185→
   186→  // Last N messages (any role), trimmed to { role, content }
   187→  const sliced = messages.slice(-outputMessages);
   188→  return sliced.map((m) => ({ role: m.role, content: m.content }));
   189→}
   190→
   191→function normalizeOptions(
   192→  rawOptions: Record<string, unknown>,
   193→  config?: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof loadTsConfig>>,
   194→  yamlExecution?: ExecutionDefaults,
   195→): NormalizedOptions {
   196→  const cliFormat = normalizeString(rawOptions.outputFormat);
   197→  const configFormat = config?.output?.format;
   198→  const formatStr = cliFormat ?? configFormat ?? 'jsonl';
   199→  const format: OutputFormat = formatStr === 'yaml' ? 'yaml' : 'jsonl';
```

   361→      map.set(evalKey, assigned);

   747→export interface RunEvalResult {
   748→  readonly executionErrorCount: number;
   749→  readonly outputPath: string;
   750→  readonly testFiles: readonly string[];
   751→  readonly target?: string;
   752→}
   753→
   754→export async function runEvalCommand(
   755→  input: RunEvalCommandInput,
   756→): Promise<RunEvalResult | undefined> {
   757→  const cwd = process.cwd();
   758→
   759→  // Load agentv.config.ts (if present) for default values
   760→  let config: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof loadTsConfig>> = null;
   761→  try {
   762→    config = await loadTsConfig(cwd);
   763→  } catch (err) {
   764→    console.warn(
   765→      `Warning: Failed to load agentv config: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}`,
   766→    );
   767→  }
   768→
   769→  const repoRoot = await findRepoRoot(cwd);
   770→
   771→  // Load .agentv/config.yaml for execution defaults.
   772→  // loadConfig expects an eval file path and walks up from its directory.
   773→  // Pass a dummy file in cwd so the search starts from the working directory.
   774→  const yamlConfig = await loadConfig(path.join(cwd, '_'), repoRoot);
   775→
   776→  // Check required_version before proceeding with eval
   777→  if (yamlConfig?.required_version) {
   778→    await enforceRequiredVersion(yamlConfig.required_version, {
   779→      strict: normalizeBoolean(input.rawOptions.strict),
   780→    });
   781→  }
   782→
   783→  let options = normalizeOptions(input.rawOptions, config, yamlConfig?.execution);
   784→
   785→  // Validate --grader-target / --model combinations
   786→  if (options.graderTarget === 'agentv' && !options.model) {
   787→    throw new Error('--grader-target agentv requires --model (e.g., --model openai:gpt-5-mini)');
   788→  }
   789→
   790→  // --retry-errors: override filter to only re-run execution_error test cases.
   791→  // IMPORTANT: JSONL must be fully loaded here, before the output writer is created below,
   792→  // since the retry source and output destination may refer to the same file.
   793→  let retryNonErrorResults: readonly EvaluationResult[] | undefined;
   794→  if (options.retryErrors) {
   795→    const retryPath = path.resolve(options.retryErrors);
   796→    await ensureFileExists(retryPath, 'Retry-errors JSONL file');
   797→    const errorIds = await loadErrorTestIds(retryPath);
   798→    if (errorIds.length === 0) {
   799→      console.log('No execution errors found in the previous output. Nothing to retry.');
   800→      return;
   801→    }
   802→    console.log(`Retrying ${errorIds.length} execution-error test(s): ${errorIds.join(', ')}`);
   803→    // Override the filter to match only error test IDs using micromatch brace expansion
   804→    const filterPattern = errorIds.length === 1 ? errorIds[0] : `{${errorIds.join(',')}}`;
   805→    options = { ...options, filter: filterPattern };
   806→    retryNonErrorResults = await loadNonErrorResults(retryPath);
   807→  }
   808→
   809→  // Validate static workspace path exists and is a directory
   810→  if (options.workspacePath) {
   811→    const resolvedWorkspace = path.resolve(options.workspacePath);
   812→    try {
   813→      const { stat } = await import('node:fs/promises');
   814→      const stats = await stat(resolvedWorkspace);
   815→      if (!stats.isDirectory()) {
   816→        throw new Error(`--workspace-path is not a directory: ${resolvedWorkspace}`);
   817→      }
   818→    } catch (err) {
   819→      if ((err as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === 'ENOENT') {
   820→        throw new Error(`--workspace-path does not exist: ${resolvedWorkspace}`);

   521→async function runWithLimit<T>(
   522→  items: readonly T[],
   523→  limit: number,
   524→  task: (item: T) => Promise<void>,
   525→): Promise<void> {
   526→  const safeLimit = Math.max(1, limit);
   527→  let index = 0;
   528→
   529→  const workers = Array.from({ length: safeLimit }, async () => {
   530→    while (index < items.length) {
   531→      const current = items[index];
   532→      index += 1;
   533→      await task(current);
   534→    }
   535→  });
   536→
   537→  await Promise.all(workers);
   538→}

   287→export function extractTotalBudgetUsd(suite: JsonObject): number | undefined {
   288→  const execution = suite.execution;
   289→  if (!execution || typeof execution !== 'object' || Array.isArray(execution)) {
   290→    return undefined;
   291→  }
   292→
   293→  const executionObj = execution as Record<string, unknown>;
   294→  const rawBudget = executionObj.total_budget_usd ?? executionObj.totalBudgetUsd;
   295→
   296→  if (rawBudget === undefined || rawBudget === null) {
   297→    return undefined;
   298→  }
   299→
   300→  if (typeof rawBudget === 'number' && rawBudget > 0) {
   301→    return rawBudget;
   302→  }
   303→
   304→  logWarning(
   305→    `Invalid execution.total_budget_usd: ${rawBudget}. Must be a positive number. Ignoring.`,
   306→  );
   307→  return undefined;
   308→}
   309→
   310→/**
   311→ * Extract `execution.fail_on_error` from parsed eval suite.
   312→ * Accepts `true` or `false`.
   313→ * Returns undefined when not specified.
   314→ */
   315→export function extractFailOnError(suite: JsonObject): FailOnError | undefined {
   316→  const execution = suite.execution;
   317→  if (!execution || typeof execution !== 'object' || Array.isArray(execution)) {
   318→    return undefined;
   319→  }
   320→
   321→  const executionObj = execution as Record<string, unknown>;
   322→  const raw = executionObj.fail_on_error ?? executionObj.failOnError;
   323→
   324→  if (raw === undefined || raw === null) {
   325→    return undefined;
   326→  }
   327→
   328→  if (typeof raw === 'boolean') {
   329→    return raw;
   330→  }
   331→
   332→  logWarning(`Invalid execution.fail_on_error: ${raw}. Must be true or false. Ignoring.`);
   333→  return undefined;
   334→}

'2024-01-01T00:00:00Z',\n    testId: 'test-1',\n    score: 1.0,\n    assertions: [{ text: 'criterion-1', passed: true }],\n    output: [{ role: 'assistant' as const, content: 'answer' }],\n    target: 0.6);\n    expect(result.passed).toBe(true);\n    expect(result.message).toContain('0.85');\n    expect(result.message).toContain('0.60');\n    expect(result.message).toContain('PASS');\n  });\n\n  it('returns FAIL when mean score is below threshold', () => {\n    const result = formatThresholdSummary(0.53, 0.6);\n    expect(result.passed).toBe(false);\n    expect(result.message).toContain('0.53');\n    expect(result.message).toContain('0.60');\n    expect(result.message).toContain('FAIL');\n  });\n\n  it('returns PASS when mean score exactly equals = formatThresholdSummary(0, 0);\n    expect(result.passed).toBe(true);\n  });\n});\n```\n\n**Step 2: Run tests to verify they fail**\n\nRun: `bun test apps/cli/test/commands/eval/threshold.test.ts`\nExpected: FAIL — `formatThresholdSummary` not found\n\n**Step 3: Implement `formatThresholdSummary` in statistics.ts**\n\nAdd to `apps/cli/src/commands/eval/statistics.ts`:\n\n```typescript\n/**\n * Format a threshold check summary line.\n * Returns whether the threshold was met and the formatted message.\n */\nexport function formatThresholdSummary(\n  meanScore: number,\n  threshold: number,\n): { passed: boolean; message: string } {\n  const passed = meanScore >= threshold;\n  const verdict = passed ? 'PASS' : 'FAIL';\n  const message = `Suite score: ${meanScore.toFixed(2)} (threshold: ${threshold.toFixed(2)}) — ${verdict}`;\n  return { passed, message };\n}\n```\n\n**Step 4: Run tests to verify they pass**\n\nRun: `bun test apps/cli/test/commands/eval/threshold.test.ts`\nExpected: PASS\n\n**Step 5: Wire the threshold check into run-eval.ts**\n\nIn `apps/cli/src/commands/eval/run-eval.ts`, after the summary is printed (around line 1153), add:\n\n```typescript\n    // Threshold quality gate check\n    const resolvedThreshold = options.threshold ?? yamlThreshold;\n    if (resolvedThreshold !== undefined) {\n      const { formatThresholdSummary } = await import('./statistics.js');\n      const thresholdResult = formatThresholdSummary(summary.mean, resolvedThreshold);\n      console.log(`\\n${thresholdResult.message}`);\n      if (!thresholdResult.passed) {\n        process.exitCode = 1;\n      }\n    }\n```\n\nNote: `yamlThreshold` needs to be captured from the `prepareEvalFile` results. Add `extractThreshold` function to `packages/core/src/evaluation/loaders/config-loader.ts` (after the `extractFailOnError` function around line 334).\n\nThe function should:\n- Extract `execution.threshold` from a parsed eval suite (JsonObject)\n- Accept a number in [0, 1] range\n- Return `undefined` when not specified or invalid\n- Use `logWarning` for invalid values (same pattern as `extractFailOnError` and `extractTotalBudgetUsd`)\n\nHere's the implementation:\n\n```typescript\n/**\n * Extract `execution.threshold` from parsed eval suite.\n * Accepts a number in [0, 1] range.\n * Returns undefined when not specified.\n */\nexport function extractThreshold(suite: JsonObject): number | undefined {\n  const execution = suite.execution;\n  if (!execution || typeof execution !== 'object' || Array.isArray(execution)) {\n    return undefined;\n  }\n\n  const executionObj = execution as Record<string, unknown>;\n  const raw = executionObj.threshold;\n\n  if (raw === undefined || raw === null) {\n    return undefined;\n  }\n\n  if (typeof raw === 'number' && raw >= 0 && raw <= 1) {\n    return raw;\n  }\n\n  logWarning(\n    `Invalid execution.threshold: ${raw}. Must be a number between 0 and 1. Ignoring.`,\n  );\n  return undefined;\n}\n```\n\n2. Add tests in `packages/core/test/evaluation/loaders/config-loader.test.ts`:\n   - Add `extractThreshold` to the import from the config-loader module (look at the existing imports at the top of the test file)\n   - Add a new `describe('extractThreshold', ...)` block after the `extractFailOnError` describe block\n\nTests to write:\n```typescript\ndescribe('extractThreshold', () => {\n  it('returns undefined when no execution block', () => {\n    const suite: JsonObject = { tests: [] };\n    expect(extractThreshold(suite)).toBeUndefined();\n  });\n\n  it('returns undefined when threshold not set', () => {\n    const suite: JsonObject = { execution: { target: 'default' } };\n    expect(extractThreshold(suite)).toBeUndefined();\n  });\n\n  it('parses valid threshold', () => {\n    const suite: JsonObject = { execution: { threshold: 0.8 } };\n    expect(extractThreshold(suite)).toBe(0.8);\n  });\n\n  it('accepts 0 as threshold', () => {\n    const suite: JsonObject = { execution: { threshold: 0 } };\n    expect(extractThreshold(suite)).toBe(0);\n  });\n\n  it('accepts 1 as threshold', () => {\n    const suite: JsonObject = { execution: { threshold: 1 } };\n    expect(extractThreshold(suite)).toBe(1);\n  });\n\n  it('returns undefined for negative threshold', () => {\n    const suite: JsonObject = { execution: { threshold: -0.1 } };\n    expect(extractThreshold(suite)).toBeUndefined();\n  });\n\n  it('returns undefined for threshold > 1', () => {\n    const suite: JsonObject = { execution: { threshold: 1.5 } };\n    expect(extractThreshold(suite)).toBeUndefined();\n  });\n\n  it('returns undefined for non-number threshold', () => {\n    const suite: JsonObject = { execution: { threshold: 'high' } };\n    expect(extractThreshold(suite)).toBeUndefined();\n  });\n});\n```\n\n3. Run the tests: `bun test packages/core/test/evaluation/loaders/config-loader.test.ts`\n\n4. But since I've given you both, you can add both and verify they pass together.\n"

Good -- `NaN` is correctly rejected because `NaN >= 0` evaluates to `false`.

Here is the full review.

---

## Code Review: `extractThreshold` (Task 1)

**Commit:** `430418a5 feat(core): add extractThreshold for execution.threshold YAML field (#698)`

**Files changed:**
- `/home/christso/projects/agentv/.worktrees/threshold-flag/packages/core/src/evaluation/loaders/config-loader.ts` (lines 336-362)
- `/home/christso/projects/agentv/.worktrees/threshold-flag/packages/core/test/evaluation/loaders/config-loader.test.ts` (lines 305-349)

**Tests:** All 69 tests pass, 0 failures.

---

### 1. Plan Alignment Analysis

The spec required:

| Requirement | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Add `extractThreshold` to `config-loader.ts` | Done | Correct file, exported function |
| Extract `execution.threshold` from JsonObject | Done | Reads from `execution` block |
| Accept number in [0, 1] range | Done | `raw >= 0 && raw <= 1` |
| Return `undefined` when not specified or invalid | Done | All invalid paths return `undefined` |
| Use `logWarning` for invalid values | Done | Warning message matches peer pattern |
| Same pattern as `extractFailOnError` / `extractTotalBudgetUsd` | Done | Identical guard structure |
| Test: no execution block | Done | |
| Test: threshold not set | Done | |
| Test: valid threshold | Done | |
| Test: 0 | Done | |
| Test: 1 | Done | |
| Test: negative | Done | |
| Test: >1 | Done | |
| Test: non-number | Done | |

**Verdict: Full alignment with the spec.** No requirements are missing and no deviations from the plan.

---

### 2. Code Quality Assessment

**What was done well:**

- The function structure precisely mirrors the established patterns in `extractTotalBudgetUsd` (line 287) and `extractFailOnError` (line 315). The guard clause (`!execution || typeof execution !== 'object' || Array.isArray(execution)`), the `as Record<string, unknown>` cast, the null/undefined early return, the validation check, and the `logWarning` fallthrough are all consistent.
- The JSDoc comment is clear and concise.
- The warning message follows the same format as peer functions: `Invalid execution.<field>: <value>. Must be <constraint>. Ignoring.`
- Tests cover all 8 cases from the spec with clean, readable assertions.
- `NaN` is implicitly handled correctly because `NaN >= 0` evaluates to `false`, so NaN values get the logWarning path. (This is a subtle edge case that works out correctly without special handling.)

---

### 3. Issues

**Important (should fix) -- 1 item:**

**Missing `snake_case` / `camelCase` alias fallback.** Every other field extraction in this file supports both wire-format (`snake_case`) and TypeScript-idiomatic (`camelCase`) keys via the `??` fallback pattern:

```typescript
// extractTotalBudgetUsd (line 294):
const rawBudget = executionObj.total_budget_usd ?? executionObj.totalBudgetUsd;

// extractFailOnError (line 322):
const raw = executionObj.fail_on_error ?? executionObj.failOnError;

// extractCacheConfig (line 276):
const cachePath = executionObj.cache_path ?? executionObj.cachePath;
```

But `extractThreshold` only reads the single key:

```typescript
const raw = executionObj.threshold;  // line 348
```

For `threshold` this is technically a non-issue because the word has no separator (it is a single word), so `snake_case` and `camelCase` are identical. **No code change is needed.** I am noting this only for completeness -- it is correct as written because there is no `camelCase` variant of a single-word key.

After further reflection, I am reclassifying this as a **non-issue**. The implementation is correct.

---

### 4. Edge Cases -- Completeness Check

The spec lists 8 test cases. All 8 are present. Let me verify there are no missing edge cases:

| Edge case | Covered? | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `execution` missing entirely | Yes | `{ tests: [] }` |
| `execution` is not an object (e.g., array) | **No test, but handled by code** | The guard `Array.isArray(execution)` on line 343 handles this |
| `threshold` is `null` | **No test, but handled by code** | Line 350: `raw === null` returns `undefined` |
| `threshold` is `NaN` | **No test, but handled by code** | `NaN >= 0` is `false`, so it falls through to `logWarning` |
| `threshold` is `Infinity` | **No test, but handled by code** | `Infinity <= 1` is `false`, falls to `logWarning` |
| `threshold` is exactly `0` | Yes | |
| `threshold` is exactly `1` | Yes | |
| `threshold` is a boolean (e.g., `true`) | **No test, but handled by code** | `typeof true === 'number'` is false |

**Suggestions (nice to have):**

- Consider adding a test for `NaN` since it is a particularly tricky JavaScript edge case for numeric validation. For example:
  ```typescript
  it('returns undefined for NaN threshold', () => {
    const suite: JsonObject = { execution: { threshold: Number.NaN } };
    expect(extractThreshold(suite)).toBeUndefined();
  });
  ```
  This is purely a documentation-of-intent concern. The code already handles it correctly.

---

### 5. Extra Functionality

There is no extra functionality beyond the spec. The implementation is minimal and focused.

---

### 6. Summary

**Verdict: Approved.** The implementation matches the spec exactly. The code follows established patterns in the file, all 8 required test cases pass, the function is correctly exported, and there is no scope creep. The one potential concern (single-key lookup without `??` alias) is actually correct behavior for a single-word field name.

The only optional improvement would be adding a `NaN` test case for extra defensive documentation, but this is not blocking.

/**
 * Format a threshold check summary line.
 * Returns whether the threshold was met and the formatted message.
 */
export function formatThresholdSummary(
  meanScore: number,
  threshold: number,
): { passed: boolean; message: string } {
  const passed = meanScore >= threshold;
  const verdict = passed ? 'PASS' : 'FAIL';
  const message = `Suite score: ${meanScore.toFixed(2)} (threshold: ${threshold.toFixed(2)}) — ${verdict}`;
  return { passed, message };
}

All done. Here is a summary of the changes made for Task 5:

---

## Summary of Changes

### 1. `apps/cli/src/commands/eval/junit-writer.ts`

- Added `JunitWriterOptions` interface with an optional `threshold` field.
- Updated the `JunitWriter` class to accept options in the constructor and `open()` method.
- Added a `threshold` instance field that defaults to `0.5` when not provided.
- Replaced all 3 hardcoded `r.score < 0.5` comparisons with `r.score < this.threshold` (in the per-suite failure count, the per-test failure element generation, and the total failure count).

### 2. `apps/cli/src/commands/eval/output-writer.ts`

- Added `WriterOptions` interface with an optional `threshold` field.
- Updated `createWriterFromPath()` to accept `WriterOptions` and pass the threshold to `JunitWriter.open()` for `.xml` files.
- Updated `createMultiWriter()` to accept `WriterOptions` and forward them to `createWriterFromPath()`.

### 3. `apps/cli/src/commands/eval/run-eval.ts`

- Moved the output writer creation from before file metadata loading to after `resolvedThreshold` is computed (line ~1016), so the resolved threshold (CLI `--threshold` or YAML `execution.threshold`) is available to pass to the JUnit writer.
- Kept the output path logging in its original position so log output order is unchanged.
- The `writerOptions` with `threshold` is passed to `createMultiWriter()` when extra output paths are present.

### 4. `apps/cli/test/commands/eval/output-writers.test.ts`

- Added test: "uses custom threshold for pass/fail when provided" -- verifies that a score of 0.9 passes and 0.6 fails when threshold is 0.8.
- Added test: "defaults to 0.5 threshold when none provided" -- verifies that a score of 0.6 passes and 0.3 fails with the default threshold.

### Verification

- All 25 tests pass in `output-writers.test.ts` (including the 2 new threshold tests).
- TypeScript typecheck passes cleanly across both `@agentv/core` and `agentv` packages.

All 1562 tests pass (1158 + 63 + 341). Now I have a comprehensive picture. Let me compile the full review.

---

## Code Review: `--threshold` Feature Implementation (Issue #698)

### Overall Assessment

The implementation is well-structured, follows existing patterns in the codebase, and all 1562 tests pass across the three packages. The feature is implemented with appropriate layering: YAML parsing in core, CLI flag in the command layer, and threshold logic in the runner. The plan documents were properly cleaned up before this review.

What was done well:
- Clean separation of concerns across the layers (core YAML parsing, CLI flag, runner logic, output writers)
- Consistent with existing `execution.*` field patterns (mirrors `extractFailOnError`, `extractTotalBudgetUsd`, etc.)
- Good test coverage for the `extractThreshold` function in core (boundary cases: 0, 1, negative, >1, non-number)
- Good test coverage for `formatThresholdSummary` (PASS, FAIL, exact equality, zero)
- JUnit writer threshold tests verify both custom and default behavior
- Non-breaking: all fields are optional, existing configurations continue to work
- `process.exit(1)` is correctly placed in the command handler (not deep in the runner)

---

### CRITICAL Issues (Must Fix)

**1. Single-path output writer does not receive `writerOptions` (threshold not passed to JUnit writer)**

In `/home/christso/projects/agentv/.worktrees/threshold-flag/apps/cli/src/commands/eval/run-eval.ts`, lines 1022-1027:

```typescript
const writerOptions =
  resolvedThreshold !== undefined ? { threshold: resolvedThreshold } : undefined;
let outputWriter: OutputWriter;
if (uniqueOutputPaths.length === 1) {
  outputWriter = await createOutputWriter(primaryWritePath, options.format);
} else {
  outputWriter = await createMultiWriter(uniqueOutputPaths, writerOptions);
}
```

When there is only a single output path (the common case), `createOutputWriter` is called **without** the `writerOptions`. This means the threshold is only passed to JUnit writer when using multiple output paths via `createMultiWriter`. For the typical single-file `-o results.xml` use case, the JUnit writer will always use the default 0.5 threshold, ignoring the user-specified value.

Additionally, `createOutputWriter` itself does not accept `WriterOptions` at all -- it dispatches by format and never creates a JUnit writer (it only supports `jsonl`, `yaml`, `html`). Users who specify `--out results.xml` would go through `createWriterFromPath` via a different path, but users with a single default output (JSONL) plus `--output results.xml` would hit the multi-writer path and work correctly. The single-path branch only applies when no `--output` flags are given, so the default JSONL writer does not need the threshold -- but if someone used `--output-format` with `.xml`, this would break.

The fix would be: when `uniqueOutputPaths.length === 1` and the path is an `.xml` file, pass `writerOptions` through. Or more robustly, also accept `WriterOptions` in `createOutputWriter`.

**Severity assessment**: This is critical for users who do `agentv eval run ... --threshold 0.7 -o results.xml` with no additional `--output` flags, as the JUnit writer would silently use 0.5 instead of 0.7. However, looking more closely at the code flow: when only `--out` is set and it ends with `.xml`, this goes through a slightly different path that builds the primary writer via format detection. Let me verify which path is actually taken.

Actually, re-reading the code more carefully: the `primaryWritePath` is always the default JSONL index file path (line ~880-895). The `extraOutputPaths` come from `--output` flags. So:

- If a user runs `agentv eval run eval.yaml --output results.xml --threshold 0.7`, then `uniqueOutputPaths = [primaryWritePath, results.xml]`, which hits the `createMultiWriter` path -- threshold IS passed. Correct.
- If a user runs `agentv eval run eval.yaml --threshold 0.7` (no explicit output), then `uniqueOutputPaths = [primaryWritePath.jsonl]`, which hits `createOutputWriter` -- no threshold needed since JSONL does not use it. Correct.
- If a user runs `agentv eval run eval.yaml --out results.xml --threshold 0.7`, then `primaryWritePath = results.xml`, `uniqueOutputPaths = [results.xml]`, which hits `createOutputWriter(results.xml, options.format)`. The `format` here would be 'jsonl' (the default) and `createOutputWriter` dispatches on format, NOT extension. So it would create a `JsonlWriter` for an `.xml` file, which is a pre-existing behavior issue, not related to this PR.

After this deeper analysis, the practical impact is minimal because the typical single-path case always produces a JSONL file which does not use threshold. The multi-writer path correctly passes threshold through. **Downgrade to Important**: the `writerOptions` variable is computed but never used in the single-path branch, which is dead code and misleading. The variable should either be used or the comment explaining the deferral should be clarified.

---

### IMPORTANT Issues (Should Fix)

**2. `writerOptions` computed but unused in single-path branch**

As analyzed above, `writerOptions` is computed at line 1020 but only used in the `else` branch (multi-writer). In the `if` branch (single path), it is silently ignored. While this happens to be safe for the current default output format (JSONL), it creates a maintenance trap. If someone later adds XML default output or changes the routing, the threshold would be silently dropped.

Recommendation: Add a brief comment or, better, pass `writerOptions` to `createOutputWriter` even if it currently does not use it (future-proofing).

**3. No input validation on CLI `--threshold` value range**

In `/home/christso/projects/agentv/.worktrees/threshold-flag/apps/cli/src/commands/eval/commands/run.ts`, lines 178-182:

```typescript
threshold: option({
  type: optional(number),
  long: 'threshold',
  description: 'Suite-level quality gate: exit 1 if mean score falls below this value (0-1)',
}),
```

The CLI accepts any number. The YAML path validates `[0, 1]` range in `extractThreshold`, but the CLI flag does not validate the range. A user could pass `--threshold 5` or `--threshold -1` and get unexpected behavior. The `normalizeOptionalNumber` function just checks for `Number.isFinite`, not the 0-1 range.

Recommendation: Add validation in `normalizeOptions` or a Zod-like constraint, or add a range check before the threshold is used in `runEvalCommand`. Something like:

```typescript
if (resolvedThreshold !== undefined && (resolvedThreshold < 0 || resolvedThreshold > 1)) {
  throw new Error('--threshold must be between 0 and 1');
}
```

**4. No documentation for the new feature in docs site or skill files**

Per AGENTS.md "Documentation Updates" section, changes to CLI commands and YAML schema should be documented in:
1. `apps/web/src/content/docs/` -- No mention of `--threshold` flag or `execution.threshold` in `running-evals.mdx` or any other docs page
2. `plugins/agentv-dev/skills/agentv-eval-writer/SKILL.md` -- No mention of `execution.threshold` field

The eval-schema.json was regenerated (good), so AI agents reading the raw schema will see the field. But human-readable docs and the skill reference card are missing this feature entirely.

**5. Unrelated changes in the diff (scope creep)**

The branch contains significant changes unrelated to the threshold feature:
- Renaming `runs` to `raw` in result layout paths (multiple files)
- Renaming `per_grader_summary` to `per_evaluator_summary` in artifact-writer
- Adding `llm-judge` / `code-judge` backward-compatibility aliases in several files
- Fixing version numbers in package.json (downgrade from 3.13.1 to 3.13.0)
- Various evaluator type alias expansions in `convert/index.ts`, `pipeline/input.ts`, `commands/prompt/accessors.ts`
- Multiple doc changes for `runs` -> `raw` directory naming

These are unrelated to issue #698 and make the PR harder to review. They should ideally be in separate commits or a separate PR.

---

### SUGGESTIONS (Nice to Have)

**6. Test coverage gap: no integration test for CLI flag overriding YAML threshold**

The tests cover:
- `extractThreshold` (core parsing) -- thorough
- `formatThresholdSummary` (formatting) -- thorough
- JUnit writer threshold behavior -- thorough

Missing:
- No test verifying that CLI `--threshold` overrides YAML `execution.threshold` (the precedence logic at line 1014-1016 of `run-eval.ts`)
- No test verifying `thresholdFailed` is returned correctly from `runEvalCommand`
- No test for the `process.exit(1)` path in the command handler

The precedence logic is simple (`options.threshold ?? yamlThreshold`), but a unit test would protect against future regressions.

**7. `calculateEvaluationSummary` uses mean from quality results only -- this is correct but worth a comment**

The threshold check at line 1178 uses `summary.mean`, which is computed from quality results only (excluding execution errors). This is the correct behavior for a quality gate, but it might be worth a one-line comment noting this, since a user might expect that execution errors cause threshold failure. The spec says "compute mean score from quality results," so this is correct.

**8. Plan documents were cleaned up**

The `docs/plans/` directory is empty, confirming the plan files were removed before this review as required by AGENTS.md. Good.

---

### Requirements Checklist

| # | Requirement | Status | Notes |
|---|-------------|--------|-------|
| 1 | `--threshold <0-1>` CLI flag on `agentv eval run` | PASS | Implemented in `run.ts` |
| 2 | `execution.threshold` in YAML eval config | PASS | Implemented in `config-loader.ts`, schema updated |
| 3 | CLI flag overrides YAML value | PASS | `options.threshold ?? yamlThreshold` at line 1016 |
| 4 | Compute mean score and compare to threshold | PASS | Uses `summary.mean` from `calculateEvaluationSummary` |
| 5 | Print "Suite score: X.XX (threshold: Y.YY) -- PASS/FAIL" | PASS | `formatThresholdSummary` in `statistics.ts` |
| 6 | JUnit writer uses threshold for per-test pass/fail (default 0.5) | PASS | `JunitWriter` constructor accepts options, defaults to 0.5 |
| 7 | Exit code 0 when score meets threshold (or no threshold set) | PASS | `thresholdFailed` only set when threshold is defined |
| 8 | Exit code 1 when score < threshold | PASS | `process.exit(1)` in command handler |

---

### Summary of Action Items

1. **Important**: Add CLI-side validation for `--threshold` range (0-1). Without this, users can pass invalid values.
2. **Important**: Add documentation for `--threshold` and `execution.threshold` in the docs site (`running-evals.mdx`) and skill reference (`SKILL.md`).
3. **Important**: Clean up the unrelated changes into separate commits/PRs, or at minimum document them clearly in the PR description.
4. **Suggestion**: Add a test verifying CLI threshold overrides YAML threshold.
5. **Suggestion**: Add a comment near the `writerOptions` code explaining why it is only needed for the multi-writer path.

  "new_string": "When halted, remaining tests are recorded with `failureReasonCode: 'error_threshold_exceeded'`. With concurrency > 1, a few additional tests may complete before halting takes effect.\n\n### Suite-Level Quality Threshold\n\nSet a minimum mean score for the eval suite. If the mean quality score falls below the threshold, the CLI exits with code 1 — useful for CI/CD quality gates.\n\n**CLI flag:**\n\n```bash\nagentv eval evals/ --threshold 0.8\n```\n\n**YAML config:**\n\n```yaml\nexecution:\n  threshold: 0.8\n```\n\nThe CLI `--threshold` flag overrides the YAML value. The threshold is a number between 0 and 1. Mean score is computed from quality results only (execution errors are excluded).\n\nWhen active, a summary line is printed after the eval results:\n\n```\nSuite score: 0.85 (threshold: 0.80) — PASS\n```\n\nThe threshold also controls JUnit XML pass/fail: tests with scores below the threshold are marked as `<failure>` in JUnit output. When no threshold is set, JUnit defaults to 0.5.\n\n## Validate Before Running"
}
```

12:The primary format. A single file contains metadata, execution config, and tests:
16:execution:
37:| `execution` | Default execution config (`target`, `fail_on_error`, etc.) |
40:| `assertions` | Suite-level evaluators appended to each test unless `execution.skip_defaults: true` is set on the test |
41:| `input` | Suite-level input messages prepended to each test's input unless `execution.skip_defaults: true` is set on the test |
75:The `assertions` field is the canonical way to define suite-level evaluators. Suite-level assertions are appended to every test's evaluators unless a test sets `execution.skip_defaults: true`.
122:To opt out for a specific test, set `execution.skip_defaults: true` (same flag that skips suite-level `assertions`).
145:Per-test `input_files` overrides the suite-level value (it does not merge). To opt out, set `execution.skip_defaults: true` on the test.
154:execution:
218:An optional YAML sidecar file provides metadata and execution config. Place it alongside the JSONL file with the same base name:
225:execution:
```

   524→
   525→```bash

   528→
   529→# Run with OTLP JSON file (importable by OTel backends)

   531→

   534→agentv prompt eval --input <file.yaml> --test-id <id>               # task input JSON (file paths, not embedded content)
   535→agentv prompt eval --expected-output <file.yaml> --test-id <id>     # expected output + evaluator criteria
   536→
   537→# Re-run only execution errors from a previous output
   538→agentv eval <file.yaml> --retry-errors <previous-output.jsonl>
   539→
   540→# Validate eval file
   541→agentv validate <file.yaml>
   542→
   543→# Compare results — N-way matrix from combined JSONL
   544→agentv compare <combined-results.jsonl>
